## Qualified installer evidence

[Gridz 1.2 qualification](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4227) pins the intact installer and its byte-identical bundled distribution terms. [The staged catalogue PR](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4232) has restored the intact archive through trusted asset promotion; a complete hosted download matches its recorded hash and size. Bounded headless browser interaction and an in-browser complete archive hash/size check are recorded in the merged entry. Launch remains disabled; sustained gameplay and save/restart remain unverified.

[Space Cab research](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/2642) supplies the original 1.2 installer source and installer-only distribution terms. The unchanged 1,820,028-byte download has SHA-256 `4c8cf772b6a08253011ae9f16312c9fe20c9a13816c96945bfdf50f6dcbe6af5`. Current installation completes 23 files, but launching the installed game returns to the launcher; gameplay and browser validation remain blocked.

## Current native runtime checkpoints

The [candidate queue](CATALOGUE-CANDIDATES.md) now includes bounded native
checkpoints collected on 9 October 2026 from the exact original archives above.
The runs use Systemless 0.83.0 with the merged absolute-path and Lo3Bytes fixes;
later runs also include the [merged MoveWindow correction](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4252).
The linked public issue notes state each run’s selection, timing and limitations.
These scripts have zero assertions: completion only means the requested actions
finished. Visible menus, alerts and splashes do not establish playable missions.

The Infocom sampler’s Zork I excerpt is identified as Release 55, serial 850823.
A [bounded command response](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4228#issuecomment-6080564528)
shows `open mailbox` revealing a leaflet and incrementing Moves to 1. This
establishes an interactive excerpt, not the full Zork I game or save/restart
qualification. Its game-data redistribution permission remains unestablished.

After [font resource swapping](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4318),
[removed-window update cleanup](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4323)
and [PtrAndHand](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4329),
Civilization II PPC Demo 1.0 was replayed on tested head
`8d072eae4489214cc6540c31b9c7715ba2f0a966` (merged in #4335) using its unchanged original
8,160,693-byte archive (SHA-256
`6ed949cc2a1b14589c28c35c7babd32bc135caff3b64e06f445a493cc0b16611`).
The bounded continuation asserts the inspected notice OK-button fill at tick
180 and clicks v=350, h=529 with a two-tick hold. The old tick-183 unsupported
PtrAndHand stop is gone; the inspected tick-362 frame shows the MacSoft splash.
The tick-962 frame shows the main menu with corrupted graphics; an unattended
tick-1562 frame is black, so a stable menu is not established by that run.
A separate interaction asserts the inspected main-menu OK fill and clicks
v=560, h=475 to accept Start a New Game. It passes through the default world
size, difficulty, civilization count, barbarian activity, standard rules,
gender, Romans, Caesar and city-style choices. Accepting the civilization
introduction produces the map interface and initial guidance at frontend/guest
tick 4644, with two assertions and no exhausted frames. The inspected image
shows red/black triangles across the terrain and red/green speckles in dialog
backgrounds; frames, icons and text are also malformed. This is
[startup progress with a rendering defect](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4331),
not proof of playable gameplay. The [PBRenameSync fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4335)
removes the former tick-4647 stop. Dismissing the initial and subsequent unit
guidance reaches a map without a modal at tick 5372. Holding Orders at v=10,
h=241 exposes an enabled Build New City item; the inspected menu has a white
background, asserted at x=376, y=26. Dragging to v=27, h=265 and releasing
reaches the default Rome name dialog at frontend tick 5556 / guest tick 5535.
Accepting Rome at v=312, h=544 reaches a city illustration/guidance dialog at
frontend tick 6158. Dismissing that dialog at v=391, h=641 reaches another
corrupted guidance dialog at frontend tick 6340 / guest tick 6319. This fresh
bounded replay passes three assertions with no exhausted frames. It establishes
menu-command and dialog responses, not completed city foundation or playable
graphics. The runtime fix implements synchronous file rename within the existing
VFS model; broader volume rename and asynchronous behavior are not established.
The sourced PPC demo does not supply the requested 68K package; exact-archive
redistribution rights, sustained gameplay, save/load, audio, Systemless hosting
and browser qualification remain unresolved. No catalogue entry is added or
enabled.

### Civilization II subsequent development replay

A newer bounded headless replay uses the same unchanged archive with committed
keyboard timing head `f30748a20c7b6c3fd5d1eac0e1d294a5382a750b`
([PR #4344](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4344)), which includes
the merged [offscreen window-list fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4343),
and **uncommitted picture callback/clipping work** under
[issue #4339](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4339). This is
local development evidence, not a claim that those graphics changes are released.

Dismissing the first-city tutorial at v=221, h=613 reveals Rome's city controls
at frontend tick 6522 / guest tick 6501. Clicking Change at v=301, h=672 opens
an inspected production chooser at frontend tick 6704 / guest tick 6683,
listing Settlers, Warriors, Phalanx, Barracks and Colossus. The prior
`LMGetKeyThresh` halt is gone. Accepting the selected Phalanx at v=570, h=590
returns to the city, and Done at v=508, h=684 returns to the map with Rome.
Return opens the Civilization Advances tutorial at frontend tick 7670 / guest
tick 7649. Dismissing that prompt at v=340, h=615 opens the research chooser
at frontend tick 8272 / guest tick 8251, with Writing selected and a visible
Science Advisor recommendation. Accepting Writing at v=476, h=289 returns to
the map at frontend tick 8874 / guest tick 8853. The inspected status panel
shows 3980 B.C., advanced from 4000 B.C., establishing the first completed
year transition. A longer scenario sends eight further bounded Return
interactions, taking a checkpoint after each. Its final inspected checkpoint
at frontend tick 13690 / guest tick 13669 shows 3860 B.C. and the Civilization
Advance dialog announcing the discovery of Writing. This establishes bounded
turn/research progress, not that each input corresponds to exactly one turn.
Dismissing the discovery details opens the next research chooser, with Code of
Laws selected and the Science Advisor recommendation visible. After accepting
it and dismissing the Writing tutorial, Command-S opens the Save File dialog
at frontend tick 16042. Clicking Save at v=339, h=518 produces the inspected
“Game saved!” confirmation for Dictator Caesar of the Romans, 3840 B.C., at
frontend tick 16644 / guest tick 16623. This is the application’s save
confirmation in the reproduction’s in-memory filesystem, not proof of
persistent export, reload or restart.
The inspected terrain, city icons and controls are substantially improved over the earlier corrupted frames.

These exploratory scenarios complete with zero assertions and zero exhausted
frames; no independent image oracle was used. They do not yet establish
full graphics correctness, sustained gameplay, save/load, audio, permission to distribute the exact archive or a
Systemless-hosted browser route. Standard QuickDraw procedure-table
initialization remains unfinished under #4339. The PowerPC demo remains distinct
from the requested 68K or dual-architecture candidate. No entry is added or enabled.

### Remaining runtime blockers

Current failures are recorded separately for [Civilization II bitmap rendering](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4331),
[Combat Mission accelerator startup](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4253),
[Worms Blast early exit](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4254),
[Oni bundled CarbonLib memory planning](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4256),
and [Unreal Tournament menu input](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4272).
Tony Hawk’s narrower version-query issue was closed as covered by the broader
[OpenGL capability work](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/3575);
that closure does not mean the public runtime implements the query.

For multi-application packages, record the selected executable. Explicitly
selecting the Aliens versus Predator game reaches its InputSprocket requirement
alert; a default-selection halt must not be attributed to that game without
resolving the selection. King of Dragon Pass requires navigating to its root
demo document from the player’s chooser to reach the title. Neither checkpoint
provides gameplay or distribution clearance.

[Additional classic demo checkpoints](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4240#issuecomment-6080877856)
record Myst Preview’s error alert and the exact Populous II demo’s prompt/title.
The latter is a different archive from the closed #1633 report; neither its
older gameplay claim nor distribution permission transfers between packages.
The current Pararena checkpoint remains Demo 2.01, and Allied General’s
initial missing-movie alert is already part of #4171’s reproduction.

The final original-demo startup checks record
[Myth’s missing installed-data alert](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/3440)
and [Alley 19’s explicit game selection](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4238#issuecomment-6081069164).
Alley 19’s bundled 68K catalogue reader does not establish a 68K game slice.

## Generic runtime follow-ups

The [temporary-memory maximum fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4298)
resolves Day of the Tentacle's former PPC TempMaxMem stop. The query shares the
actual handle allocator's side-effect-free allocation plan, including the master
pointer, reusable blocks, reserved mappings and allocation limits. All 85 PPC
Memory Manager and 116 common process-context tests pass; the new regressions
verify allocation of the reported maximum and failure at one extra byte.

The unchanged original PPC demo displays its opening spiral at frontend/guest
tick 180, the LucasArts logo at 780, the company caption at 1380, and animated
footage with the two tentacles at 3180. A separate classic 68K replay of the same
archive displays the opening logo at frontend tick 180 / reported guest tick
780. The reports identify their respective architectures; these are separate
route checks, not a timing comparison. Named captures were inspected. The
bounded scenarios complete with zero automatic assertions, no guest halt and
no exhausted frame budgets. The original README describes a non-interactive
demonstration; full playback, audio fidelity and browser qualification remain
unverified. The archive is 1,640,963 bytes with SHA-256
`cb5719f0b4b8a5f96806e9359a6fc6f266eb134ca871de0bf92860b7437e6394`;
exact-archive redistribution permission remains unestablished.

The unchanged Alpha Centauri 1.4b6 PPC demo reaches its welcome dialog at
frontend/guest tick 780 and waits there through 1380. A two-tick click at
(v 493, h 273), followed by 180 ticks, renders its main menu at 1562. Selecting
Quick Start at (v 193, h 604) and running 600 ticks reaches the Gaian Planetfall
introduction at 2164. Dismissing it at (v 571, h 582) and running 600 ticks reaches
the first-base naming prompt at 2766. Accepting Gaia's Landing at (v 169, h 399)
and running 180 ticks reaches the first-base welcome panel at 2948 after the
[MathLib hypot](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4305) and
[acos fixes](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4307).

Closing the welcome panel at (v 570, h 250) and running 60 ticks reaches the active
map at 3010. Clicking Turn Complete at (v 415, h 677) and running 180 ticks opens
the Operations Director confirmation at 3192. Selecting Yes at (v 196, h 397),
running 30 ticks, then confirming OK at (v 225, h 397) and running 600 ticks
returns to the map at 3826 showing Mission Year 2102, Energy 12 and Turn Complete.
Each click holds the button for two frontend ticks. The
[synchronous rename fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4312)
resolves the former null call to weak `InterfaceLib:PBHRenameSync` at frontend
3253 / guest 3252. This replay also passed on exact PR revision
`21afa9142dae2dff3593c8cbceffc22d51ac395c` after rebasing the newer public
Menu/Help Manager changes. Named captures were inspected; no halted guest or
exhausted frame budget was reported. These zero-assertion checks establish a
bounded native first-turn interaction, not sustained gameplay, save/load,
audio fidelity or browser qualification. The original archive remains
18,267,807 bytes with SHA-256
`2ab4f1de7291d3704b08d7b12dd659ef3b4fbab4b10914f31b02c2ccec931191`;
exact-archive redistribution permission remains unestablished.

The SimFarm source recheck on 10 October 2026 (Sydney) covered the issue-linked
Macintosh Garden [disk package](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/802#issuecomment-6085271274)
and [CD package](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/802#issuecomment-6086209908).
Fresh primary signed downloads returned HTTP 410; both listed mirrors returned

archive receipt or redistribution grant. These results cover only the inspected

[Temporary handle locking](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4264)
and [uncached memory copying](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4267)
have merged. The [menu command-ID fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4277)

unchanged Heroes III original demo also passes its former SetMenuItemRefCon
stop after the [reference-constant fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4283)

one API with Apple's documented nondebugging-build behavior; all 64 PPC
DrawSprocket tests and public CI checks pass.

The [AIFF-header fix](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4294) resolves
SoundLib:ParseAIFFHeader against the open file and parses bounded AIFF/AIFC
metadata. All 42 PPC sound-manager tests pass. The unchanged original music
files use stereo 16-bit ima4 data at 22,050 Hz; compressed COMM frame counts
and first-sample offsets are retained without decoding their samples.

`3e5f77588f00ca681717aeaed8060d1e400a1774fb6c9787232209d64c007680`;

## Avara expansion intake

Tracked under [catalogue expansion #4353](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4353). Retrieved 10 October 2026 from the [VintageAppleMac A games index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/a/). These are exact download receipts, not a redistribution approval.

| Distribution | Bytes | SHA-256 | Inspection boundary |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| [Avara Installer.sit](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Avara%20Installer.sit) | 2325085 | `917934ea0c9bc1b004ad7a97943c238634f9d72d7077d801844e31d7c93c1e5e` | StuffIt 5 contains one installer named Avara Installer; data fork 2,294,140 bytes. Installer CODE resources do not establish game architecture. Installed-game licence and complete payload still require inspection. |
| [Avara 1.0.1.sit](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Avara%201.0.1.sit) | 2476740 | `136f1dff7198bbf4e0896223d4af462269610bbefea8547b48d657b6fd33ccfe` | Outer archive contains a VISE installer named Avara 1.0.1. The current public loader expands 132 files, including the game, documentation, licence, mission data and editing materials. See inspection and bounded runtime evidence below. |

Both unchanged downloads are local scratch files; neither has been staged for hosting. The original author's [MIT source release](https://github.com/jmunkki/Avara) establishes a source-code lead, not permission for every component of these historical distributions. Screenshot qualification, CI asset promotion, hosted browser approval and live publication are unverified.

**1.0.1 payload inspection:** `Documentation ƒ/Avara License.text` is 2,469 bytes with SHA-256 `198db82577e60e4df0eef7f6f9376015f8a9270dc65aff2f9b3e3149b0bd34e0`. It permits nonprofit distribution of the complete unmodified software, excludes for-profit distribution without written permission, and retains the 30-day trial/registration requirement. This is a conditional historical distribution grant, not freeware or a licence to remove the shareware notice. Remaining bundled terms and completeness must be checked before promotion. `Avara 1.0.1 Notes.text` is 538 bytes, SHA-256 `77d0e0f0570bef6e7df4b6956556c78404a32c7084e504d7833abe08882df6c4`, and dates the update to 20 October 1999.

**Executable evidence:** the installed game has 17 CODE resources and four `pwpc` PEF acceleration modules in PLUG resources 128, 129, 385 and 641. Both CPU-preference startup probes report actual architecture 68k. Use exact executable selection `Avara 1.0.1/Avara 1.0.1 ƒ/Avara`; the shorter override `Avara` selects the bundled Avara Commuter utility. Do not transfer that utility's startup evidence to the game.

**Bounded runtime:** current runtime at `a6571ed4` reaches the game's shareware notice at frontend tick 180 / guest tick 780. Dismissing Not Yet reaches player setup; typing a player name and starting the server reaches the mission roster. Game → Start produces a blue horizon without an available HECTOR at frontend tick 976 / guest tick 1554. Space and 180 additional ticks leave that state visible. All runs have zero assertions and zero instruction-budget exhaustion, so they establish inspected checkpoints, not qualified gameplay or an indefinite hang. Investigate mission selection/spawn semantics in [#4355](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4355); architecture-specific gameplay remains unverified.


**Mission parser diagnosis:** the original author's [pinned `ConvertToLevelMap` implementation](https://github.com/jmunkki/Avara/blob/1e64a866c430cc0e44c60c873c0e36d90895e126/src/Avara/GameParser/LevelLoader.c#L406) installs custom QuickDraw arc, round-rect, polygon, text, rectangle, oval, comment and picture-input procedures in a 2×1 offscreen GWorld. These callbacks construct game objects and execute level scripts. The unchanged archive's picture trace reaches a 32-bit 2×1 port with a large mission picture frame, but the current 68K DrawPicture path delegates directly to the host decoder without calling the live guest procedure table; picture comments are skipped. This explains the empty-world checkpoint structurally, without claiming a complete callback-entry execution trace. Track the general callback fix in [#4355](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4355); [#4339](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4339) concerns related PPC picture bit-transfer callbacks. Do not replace Avara's guest parser with host-generated objects.

## Barrack expansion intake

Inspected 10 October 2026. The bundled `Documentation ƒ/Barrack License.text` expressly permits complete, unchanged nonprofit distribution without prior written notice, prohibits distribution for profit without written permission, and retains the 30-day trial and registration requirement. This is a conditional archive grant, not freeware. Additional documentation was inspected for distribution and licensing provisions: the Ambrosia FAQ, product FAQ, Barrack FAQ, release notes and contest notice retain shareware registration requirements without replacing the bundled complete unchanged nonprofit grant. Preserve all eighteen files and the original trial; this is not a freeware release.

Explicit executable selection was `Barrack 1.0.4/Barrack 1.0.4 ƒ/Barrack`. The existing native 0.83.0 diagnostic binary produced a black 68K startup capture at frontend tick 180. The default route reports **PPC**; its tick-780 capture is black except for a small yellow outlined horizontal rectangle. Both scripts had zero assertions; neither capture establishes gameplay. No catalogue manifest, screenshot asset, promotion or publication is claimed. Keep this candidate in intake and assess alternatives before undertaking runtime fixes.

## Chiral expansion intake

Inspected 10 October 2026 from the [VintageAppleMac C index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/c/). The intact [Chiral 1.0.4 archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Chiral%201.0.4.sit) is **1,592,421 bytes**, SHA-256 `1e8283345e60a6a84a86f6121c2616df4e835d61e49944223823455b4f83e226`. Its sole initial file is the original installer, with a 1,561,484-byte data fork and 52,175-byte resource fork. The alternative [Chiral.sit](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Chiral.sit) is 1,567,566 bytes, SHA-256 `eaed1f4de9d125d91fbcc46ca127713226f2f9dc60638a261e9dc7cda3b5ad77`; it also contains an installer and is not treated as the same archive.

Native 0.83.0, classic 68K preference, reaches the installer at frontend tick 180. Clicking Install (v378/h565) and allowing another 780 ticks reaches the explicit successful-installation dialog at tick 962, naming `Chiral 1.0.4 ƒ` and its Notes, Chiral FAQ and Ambrosia FAQ files. The shorter tick-482 checkpoint still showed the final registration application being installed. These screenshots were inspected; the scripts had zero assertions and prove only this bounded installation flow. Installed application execution, bundled licence inspection, game architecture, gameplay and browser/pipeline stages remain pending. No game manifest has been submitted.

**Installed contents follow-up:** A scratch runner replayed installation and exported the created VFS files for inspection. Fourteen files were installed beneath `Chiral 1.0.4 ƒ`, including the game, registration application, licence, FAQs, tutorial and all media. The 2,348-byte `Chiral License` grants complete unchanged nonprofit distribution, excludes distribution for profit without written permission, and retains the 30-day trial. Notes date 1.0.4 to 30 April 1996 and describe the game as fat since 1.0.2. The game has a 75,253-byte data fork and 104,682-byte resource fork. Remaining FAQ/tutorial terms need review.

The original installer exits via ExitToShell after its completion dialog; the headless scenario therefore terminates at frontend tick 1233 rather than launching the game. For diagnosis only, a temporary ZIP of MacBinary-wrapped installed forks was created outside the repository. Explicit selection of `Chiral 1.0.4 ƒ/Chiral` on the 68K route reaches a visibly rendered Chiral title at tick 180. This is a derived diagnostic package, **not** the original archive and not a proposed distribution. It does not establish original-archive gameplay or qualification. A supported original-installer-to-installed-game launch path, both executable slices, bounded gameplay and browser validation remain necessary.

**Direct original release intake:** The unchanged [Chiral 1.0.0 ƒ.sit](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Chiral%201.0.0%20%C6%92.sit) is 1,043,239 bytes, SHA-256 `b4c289ac4e8ee67836281d94ce54a9a724a49802106adb49a57741c5570b81d3`. It expands ten original files, including the game, licence, Notes, Ambrosia Products and all game media. The licence permits unchanged complete nonprofit distribution. Its unlimited-use sentence conflicts with the displayed 30-day shareware notice; retain both and do not claim the archive is freeware. Notes identify the original April 1994 release. This is a separate archive from the 1.0.4 installer.

Explicit `Chiral 1.0.0 ƒ/Chiral` selection on 68K reaches the shareware prompt at tick 780. Not Yet (v424/h399) opens the menu at tick 902; Play Chiral (v154/h488) opens Level 1 at tick 1084, then the board at 1384. Clicking v290/h300 places the purple atom on the board and advances the dispenser to a red atom at tick 1446. Inspected screenshots establish bounded interaction, not level completion or sustained play. `catalogue/chiral.md` stages this original archive and the full game-content framebuffer with launch disabled. Hosting, browser approval and live deployment remain pending.

Don't Change (v398/h411) leads through the publisher splash to the shareware notice at tick 1562. Not Yet (v337/h490) displays the main menu at 1684. New Game (v285/h320) starts a visibly rendered bubble maze at 1866, with the fish, gems, lives and score. **PPC follow-up:** Dismissing the Sound Manager warning with OK (v350/h494), then Don't Change (v398/h412), Not Yet (v337/h490) and New Game (v285/h320), starts the actual PPC maze. Holding Right for 30 ticks moves Blinky along the corridor at frontend tick 2078. The maze background is black instead of the texture visible on 68K; bounded movement works but graphical fidelity is incomplete. Both zero-assertion screenshots were inspected. The bundled Ambrosia FAQ encourages sharing subject to the evaluation period; the READ ME retains music credits and describes the fat application. ## Hosted browser CORS qualification blocker

The temporary local browser preview built from Chiral PR revision `399e0c95d8f96cf3ab12f2a1e4a4149d4993f552` uses both promoted R2 sources with launch enabled only in the temporary build. Tracked manifests were restored immediately after building and remain disabled. On the Chiral route, the actual browser reports `FETCH FAILED: TYPEERROR: FAILED TO FETCH`. HTTP GET with Origin `http://127.0.0.1:8091` independently returns the correct archive body but lacks Access-Control-Allow-Origin. No browser gameplay is claimed.

The existing [R2 CORS workflow run](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/38029053084) validates the repository policy and fails applying it with authentication error 10000. The deployment token is recognized but cannot manage the bucket CORS policy. The maintainer has been asked to update that credential or apply `www/r2-cors.json` directly; no secret value was requested. Public-read CORS must be applied and real browser fetching/input retested before launch approval. This blocks these browser gates, not all catalogue research or native compatibility work.

Current production extraction yields sixty files, and single-/multi-source browser packs are identical at 27,516,094 bytes. This is not an independent all-fork comparison.

The bundled 2,457-byte Ares licence permits unchanged complete nonprofit distribution, excludes for-profit distribution without written permission and retains the 30-day trial. The included GameRanger Beta Read Me points to its bundled 3,185-byte Terms and Conditions of Use. Those terms permit intact, unchanged GameRanger archive distribution but separately require prior permission for bundling with a product/service or CD-ROM. Applicable permission for mirroring the complete Ares bundle remains unestablished; the game licence alone does not clear it. Other auxiliary terms remain to be reviewed. Do not remove bundled components to bypass the complete-package condition; unhosted.

Selector `Ares 1.1.1/Ares 1.1.1 ƒ/Ares`, PowerPC, depth 8, native exact head `39bd994b91a4fb2ba38886c0da73f246a9fa8464`: the merged [special-handler fix #4367](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4367) resolves the old first halt. A bounded 600-tick attempt now loads ObjectSupportLib proc resources and stops on unsupported `InterfaceLib:AEInstallCoercionHandler`, PC `01F00A08`, SP `07F6FF10`, LR `030220B0`, before the requested capture. [Current evidence](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4364#issuecomment-6105524171) records the startup failure and terms review; gameplay and browser qualification remain incomplete.

## Monkey Shines expansion intake

The unchanged [Monkey Shines 1.1.2 archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Monkey%20Shines%201.1.2.sit), linked from the [M index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/m/), is 3684474 bytes, SHA-256 `e41df59a63aed3127f344435c4cc65b48f5212b227c727f427c4d19157dac045`. The loader expands 13 files, including five original world files, game, optional editor, README, editor manual and order form. The original README expressly allows free web/FTP distribution of the unregistered version with all original files unchanged; it retains the 30-day trial and limits unregistered play to the first world. The archive's registration state and remaining document terms require confirmation before hosting.

Explicit `Monkey Shines 1.1.2/Monkey Shines` selection on the actual PPC route stops at unsupported `InterfaceLib:PBResolveFileIDRefSync`, frontend tick 1 / guest tick 0, PC `01F002F8`, LR `010116BC`; tracked in [#4366](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4366). The separate actual 68K route loads pictures and reaches an inspected trial notice over the menu at frontend tick 1980. Attempts to dismiss Not Yet at v443/h250 produce black intermediate captures; a later Return displays an Enter Code dialog. These actions do not establish dismissal, world selection or gameplay. No registration code is entered and no playable claim is made.

### Monkey Shines original trial button investigation

The original README, editor manual, order form and bug-report document have now been reviewed. The unregistered package explicitly permits free website/FTP distribution with every original file retained unchanged; its 30-day trial and first-world restriction remain applicable. The running original displays its unregistered trial notice. No registration code, modified executable or repacked distribution is used.

A fresh original-archive 68K replay waits 3,000 frontend ticks before pressing Not Yet. Read-only debugger evidence establishes that FindWindow returns the trial window, GlobalToLocal produces local `(310, 59)`, and FindControl returns button part 10. The subsequent TrackControl call incorrectly supplies the original global `(443, 250)` instead of the local point. Its control rectangle is `(300, 14, 320, 104)`. This explains the rejected click; it is not a lost host input event. [Issue #4427](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4427) records the exact original package and diagnostic evidence.

The compatibility change accepts this legacy global-point calling pattern only for a standard button when the supplied point exactly matches the live held mouse and its converted local point hits that control. Correct local points, arbitrary misses, unheld input, scrollbars and other control definitions retain their existing handling. The 68K regression verifies both inside release and outside-release cancellation; ten existing/updated TrackControl tests and the architecture-neutral boundary regression pass. A rebuilt native runner replays the intact original 68K package: the held button and post-release captures are inspected, and Not Yet dismisses to the illustrated menu at frontend tick 3,332, with no exhausted budgets. This scenario has zero gameplay assertions; clicking the New Game label does not establish world entry. World gameplay, browser qualification and catalogue publication remain pending. The original PPC route currently reaches the trial notice over a blank menu; artwork and input still require independent qualification.

### Monkey Shines custom control and native gameplay

[Issue #4431](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4431) records the separate custom New Game control. Its guest CDEF returns part 10 for the correct local `(180, 64)` icon hit, but TrackControl receives global `(240, 144)`. The compatibility change at `40af948aa013a0959a789a4d4cf6ce0a027f6c5a` retains nil-action custom tracking, dispatches the original CDEF for hit testing and drawing, samples release in the owner's local coordinates, preserves the initial custom part and restores the original highlight. The same guarded live global-point conversion applies before the custom hit test. It does not replace that test with a rectangle or force button part 10.

All twelve TrackControl regressions pass. The new runner regression executes real 68K callback instructions and verifies local arguments, custom part 42, initial no-hit, a different release part, cancellation, pressed drawing, restoration, callback counts and Pascal stack cleanup. The native debug-server build passes. Replaying the unchanged original 13-file archive on 68K now dismisses Not Yet, opens world selection with registered worlds locked, selects Spooked and enters its first room. Right-arrow movement, up-arrow jumping and enemy animation are visible in inspected captures. A repeat passes seven measured pixel assertions at frontend tick 5,906 / guest tick 6,368, with zero exhausted frame budgets. Room completion, key collection, sustained play, saves, audio and editor are not claimed.

The new catalogue draft uses the complete original archive and this actual gameplay capture, with launch disabled pending browser qualification and normal asset promotion. The original PPC slice remains unqualified for trial input and menu artwork; it is not advertised as supported by this draft. Native gameplay does not establish browser or live delivery.

The normal promotion job [38076668356](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/38076668356) validates 198 entries but receives HTTP 403 from the original source mirror. The complete unchanged trial is therefore supplied through the existing [intake release](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/releases/download/catalogue-intake-20261010/monkey-shines-112.sit). Independently downloaded bytes match the original SHA-256 and 3,684,474-byte receipt; CI still performs the normal immutable asset promotion. No archive repacking or rights change is involved.

A current-build actual PPC retest reproduces a read MemoryFault twice, with default FAT selection and explicit PPC preference: frontend tick 3,003 / guest tick 6,002, PC `0100DD78`, address `AC420004`, LR `0100DD4C`, after the trial mouse-down. The inspected failure capture retains the trial over missing menu artwork. Zero exhausted budgets and zero assertions do not qualify gameplay. [Issue #4434](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4434) records both runs and the exact callsite; no regression or root cause is inferred from this observation.

### Monkey Shines browser launch qualification

The optimized local release preview on build `53faf2c5dff8d9db77136a301767d61b1f83ed48` uses the normal Chrome catalogue player: worker=true, 68K, WebGL, default 25 MHz pacing, 800×600 and crossOriginIsolated=true. Original Not Yet dismissal, New Game icon, Spooked entry, walking and an airborne jump respond to ordinary browser mouse and arrow-key events in inspected per-step captures. Local preview delivery supplies independently hash-verified original bytes to the upstream archive request. No runtime exception or loading error is observed; the owned qualification session exits cleanly. [Browser evidence](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4431#issuecomment-6100999269).

[Normal promotion run 38077118707](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/38077118707) succeeds and commits immutable references as `0792ba02be7e4804c8ce0d5b9a6b5268eee3220f`. The independently downloaded [Systemless-managed original archive](https://assets.systemless.org/catalogue/objects/sha256/e4/e41df59a63aed3127f344435c4cc65b48f5212b227c727f427c4d19157dac045.sit) matches the pinned SHA-256 and 3,684,474-byte receipt. The actual gameplay screenshot is SHA-256 `c8671bcc8a2acc7d528b82c437fb5aa071ced366540ac4e5a143ec6f6673babc`, 294,594 bytes. Production validation passes 198 entries. The promoted 68K entry now enables launch; final exact-head CI, release and an unmodified actual public-route replay remain pending. This is browser launch qualification, not yet live delivery. PPC remains tracked in #4434.

## MacSki expansion intake

The unchanged [MacSki v1.7 archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/MacSki%20v1.7.sit) is 896089 bytes, SHA-256 `57b0b5b079c9fa9c7e1b236809511a5e0a680a1bd3447a1e87617bc1f2e95791`. The loader expands 43 files: game, colour art, sounds and courses. No standalone distribution licence appears in that listing; rights remain unverified. The native 68K startup displays “MacSki is damaged” at ticks 180 and 780. This is the application's diagnostic, not proof of an infected or damaged source archive. Gameplay is unqualified; resource validation needs diagnosis.

The actual native PPC route of this 1.7 archive stops at unsupported `InterfaceLib:LMGetHiliteRGB`, PC `01F00100`, caller `01023048`, before gameplay. The accessor defect is tracked in [#4425](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4425).

A stronger publication candidate is the creator-hosted final [MacSki 1.7.2 ZIP](https://www.robotroom.com/StormImpact/MacSki_v172.zip), linked by [David Cook's original Storm Impact page](https://www.robotroom.com/StormImpact.html). A fresh fetch is 909073 bytes, SHA-256 `9c4598565ce07414f4c78b37354426ffbe6ced9df8aa23c317d920ffd5884e1e`; the loader expands 45 files, including version history and main `MacSki_ v1.7.2/MacSki v1.7.2`. Its 196828-byte data fork and 434703-byte resource fork remain unchanged. The native 68K route on runtime source `5d3babbd` displays the same integrity warning at frontend tick 300. This independent creator download makes a mirror-specific damaged archive less likely; it does not identify the emulator defect. The creator describes checksum-based integrity checks and proprietary resource compression. The final version's full embedded Distribution and Legal notices, PPC startup and gameplay, and browser qualification remain pending. The [previously decoded 1.6 restriction](#macski-distribution-restriction-decoded) prohibits mass distribution outside its specified BBS exception; the final version must establish applicable website permission before publication. No launch approval or redistribution permission is inferred solely from the creator's download link.

The highlight accessor implementation for [#4425](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4425) resolves both get/set APIs through InterfaceLib and CarbonLib, copies the shared system preference using checked six-byte buffers, and seeds the same default as QuickDraw. The ABI regression and four existing keyboard-preference regressions pass; production validation passes 197 records. A rebuilt native 0.91.1 runner on base `e7653a23` plus this fix replays both unchanged 1.7 and creator-hosted 1.7.2 archives on actual PPC through frontend/guest tick 300, with zero exhausted frame budgets. Inspected captures display the original integrity warning; the script has zero gameplay assertions. The unsupported import is fixed, while the integrity check, gameplay and final-version website permission remain unresolved.

## MacSki distribution restriction decoded

The original v1.6 executable's compressed `TEXT` 9000 resource declares 7240 decoded bytes. Inspection of the decompressed resource in guest memory recovered that full length without changing the archive. It encourages complete, unchanged sharing with friends, family, user groups and BBS services, preserving courses, sounds and colour art. It also prohibits commercial distribution and mass distribution of at least 50 copies in any twelve-month period, except its specified BBS arrangement; CD distribution is prohibited. Registration codes and the manual may not be redistributed. Permission for public website distribution at our intended scale is therefore unresolved. Hold publication pending applicable affirmative permission; do not treat the BBS exception as permission for a website. The native skiing receipt above remains compatibility evidence only.

## Monkey Shines PPC startup fix

[PR #4370](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4370) implements synchronous `PBResolveFileIDRefSync` using existing file IDs, mounted-volume boundaries and checked output buffers. All 180 local File Manager tests pass, including two new regression tests. The unchanged 1.1.2 archive now reaches its original unregistered trial notice on actual PPC at frontend tick 1980. A subsequent held Not Yet click produces a black capture at tick 2610; world gameplay remains unqualified. This fixes the unsupported startup import, not all game compatibility. The inspected README permits complete, unchanged, free web/FTP trial distribution; no registration code was entered.

The public R2 response still lacks the required CORS header. A temporary local preview redirected only the exact Chiral and Bubble Trouble archive URLs in its compiled WASM to same-length localhost URLs serving SHA-256-verified original bytes. Guest code and archives were unchanged; this is a local diagnostic, not production approval. Chiral then fetched successfully and displayed its original trial notice. A Not Yet click remained visibly pressed; dismissal and browser gameplay remain unqualified. Investigate input delivery independently of the public CORS fix. No tracked launch flag was enabled.


## Chiral browser gameplay and input isolation

The local mirror test subsequently reached the original unregistered menu, started Level 1 and accepted a green atom on the board; the dispenser advanced to a red atom. The full browser-context capture has SHA-256 `62911cdae278c9cc8f0133c40d90bcef80b418cbc4d96cf34b670f07484a5209`. The tested WASM runtime remains revision `399e0c95d8f9`, version 0.83.0. The mirror served the already-recorded exact original archive; observational worker diagnostics changed no guest executable or trial state. This proves bounded local browser input response, not sustained play, level completion, production fetching or launch approval.

Stationary Not Yet clicks, including a 500 ms hold, leave the button pressed. The host posts both down and up at v425/h400; observational diagnostics subsequently show no pressed worker input and no pending releases while guest UI tracking remains active. A short pointer drag entirely within the button closes the notice. [Issue #4373](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4373) records this behavior independently of a fix. Native GUI-slice comparisons dismiss the notice with a one-frame hold, including a 64-instruction-slice variant and a separately exercised audio-mixing variant. These comparisons do not establish the root cause or verify a current-master WASM build.

The captured stationary-click failure has SHA-256 `a31ab39c5c0a48cddb538636158b79f520cecfc15efbecf91a0cc77b7465e19d`. Captures and scratch scripts remain outside tracked source. A production-host test after the CORS correction, a current-runtime input retest, and the repository launch-approval process are still required. The original archive's 30-day trial notice was retained; no registration code was entered.

## Original UDIF loading compatibility fix

[#4377](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4377) tracks recognition and bounded decoding of original compressed DMGs. Before this fix, the unchanged official [Bugdom Mac OS 9 package](https://www.pangeasoft.net/bug/files/Bugdom.dmg) (`50313726` bytes, SHA-256 `be0f8f01164017007f12a5955183e2b19db4a4584615daddde9ee133c8be9ea7`) was incorrectly parsed as MacBinary and reported no executable. The official [Nanosaur 1.3.4 package](https://www.pangeasoft.net/nano/Nanosaur.dmg) (`14376561` bytes, SHA-256 `0cda02a8caceae0c2d9165f9fdb019d01be7312cd87afbf7f57cb07d69f80b5f`) reported truncated MacBinary. Both contain bzip2-compressed UDIF block tables and HFS+ game contents.

The decoder reconstructs 86660096 bytes for Bugdom and 31613952 bytes for Nanosaur. Every sector retained by macOS's read-only `hdiutil` UDTO conversion matches byte for byte; the final ten sectors, omitted by that conversion, are declared Apple_Free and decode to zero. Conversion is an independent inspection comparison only: the runtime tests load the original DMGs directly, and no derived image is proposed for hosting. Raw, zlib, bzip2 and sparse chunks are supported; unsupported compression, invalid ranges/counts, overlapping or missing chunks, and incorrect decoded sizes are rejected. Extraction is limited to a 256 MiB disk and a 16 MiB XML table. The catalogue pipeline recognizes original `.dmg` artifacts and retains its existing provenance/promotion gates.

Explicit PPC selection of `HFS+ Disk Image/Bugdom™ (Shareware)` from the original DMG reaches the unregistered Demo notice at frontend tick 780. Clicking Demo at v344/h361 for two ticks reaches its missing-3D-acceleration warning by tick 1562. Explicit PPC selection of `HFS+ Disk Image/Nanosaur™` reaches its original unregistered notice and, after Demo at v284/h281 for two ticks, its dinosaur title splash by tick 1562. Both reports complete the requested actions with zero instruction-budget exhaustion and zero assertions; inspected screenshots establish these visible startup states, not gameplay. Nanosaur's extended run reaches a black capture at tick 3362. A shorter Bugdom continuation acknowledges the hardware warning (v364/h320, held for two ticks) and reaches a textured 3D title scene by tick 1624; this is title rendering, not interactive gameplay. A native stack sample during the longer continuation spends 345 of 419 sampled main-thread stacks inside completed QuickDraw 3D frame rendering. The longer diagnostic was stopped and replaced with the bounded title capture. Neither title is qualified for launch.

Original-archive redistribution remains held. Bugdom's demo notice is not an affirmative whole-package redistribution grant. Nanosaur's original ReadMe licence restricts distribution/network transmission. Permissions for publisher-endorsed modern source ports are not applied to these original PPC binaries. This fix advances compatibility without publishing either archive or overriding rights/launch requirements.


## v0.84.0 production route verification

The release workflow's website deployment succeeded for tag `v0.84.0`, revision `4ef55cf313675e62b113e2b473df8129389d36a2`. Browser inspection verified both [Chiral](https://systemless.org/chiral/) and [Bubble Trouble](https://systemless.org/bubble-trouble/) routes, their original managed download links and the v0.84.0 footer. Both explicitly state that launching is not enabled. This proves publication of catalogue pages, not newly launch-qualified games.

A fresh GET of the exact Chiral object with `Origin: https://systemless.org` returns HTTP 200, the recorded 1043239-byte archive hash and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://systemless.org`. A separate GET with the localhost preview origin returns no CORS header. The earlier blanket missing-header blocker is therefore no longer accurate for production. Public-runtime fetching still needs a browser test after the launch gate is properly approved; local-origin fetching remains restricted. The earlier failed repository policy workflow is not evidence that this current policy was applied through that workflow.

## Original Pangea Mac OS 9 intake

The publisher's [Bugdom download page](https://www.pangeasoft.net/bug/downloads.html) links [Bugdom.dmg](https://www.pangeasoft.net/bug/files/Bugdom.dmg): 50313726 bytes, SHA-256 `be0f8f01164017007f12a5955183e2b19db4a4584615daddde9ee133c8be9ea7`. It identifies the original OS 9 game as limited to demo mode until registered. The [Nanosaur files page](https://www.pangeasoft.net/nano/files.html) separately links the original OS 9 1.3.4 [Nanosaur.dmg](https://www.pangeasoft.net/nano/Nanosaur.dmg): 14376561 bytes, SHA-256 `0cda02a8caceae0c2d9165f9fdb019d01be7312cd87afbf7f57cb07d69f80b5f`.

Both are bzip2-compressed UDIF images. Before the [UDIF fix #4380](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4380), the loader misidentified their original bytes as MacBinary: Nanosaur reports a truncated container; Bugdom reports no executable and interprets compressed bytes as a spurious file. [Issue #4377](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4377) records that original-container defect; the direct-original retest above supersedes this initial expanded-copy diagnosis. Temporary `hdiutil convert -format UDTO` copies outside source expose their HFS+ contents: 44 files for Nanosaur and 121 for Bugdom, including original PPC PEF executables and separate resource forks. These converted copies are local inspection artifacts and are not proposed distributions.

Actual PPC startup of the expanded Nanosaur copy reaches the original unregistered notice at frontend tick 780; the Bugdom copy reaches its corresponding notice at tick 180. Exact selectors are `HFS+ Disk Image/Nanosaur™` and `HFS+ Disk Image/Bugdom™ (Shareware)`. Native binary version 0.83.0 includes the file-ID fix from #4370. Startup notices alone do not qualify gameplay, browser behavior or the compressed original package.

Nanosaur's original included `Read Me First` is a software licence that restricts redistribution and network transmission; publication needs applicable affirmative permission. Its publisher-endorsed modern port's licence is not being extended to this original PPC package. Bugdom's inspected English instructions identify copyrights for game art and music but do not establish an archive redistribution grant. Neither package is cleared for hosting, and no registration code is supplied.


On the expanded copies, choosing the original **Demo** button (Nanosaur v284/h281, Bugdom v344/h361, two-tick hold) advances both applications. At frontend tick 1562, Nanosaur displays its dinosaur title screen; Bugdom displays a warning that no 3D hardware accelerator was found and offers to continue. Both reports select actual PPC, have zero budget-exhausted frames and zero assertions. These inspected captures establish demo-flow progress only; neither establishes a playable 3D scene. Game and image contents remain unchanged, and no registration code is entered.


## Pac game expansion intake

The unchanged [Pac the Man 4.0.1e archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Pac%20the%20Man%204.0.1e.sit), linked from the [P index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/p/), is **3,112,344 bytes**, SHA-256 `c36e5dafd67733d9ec5802ae4ad40bd4df95a84f24dd0e0f7dfdd79a0769c9a6`. It expands eight files: the fat application, level resources, original README, four optional songs and their README. The original README calls the game freeware but gives no explicit redistribution grant; the music README supplies installation instructions only. Hosting permission remains unverified. Actual 68K reaches the colourful title/high-score screen at frontend tick 780, guest tick 1380. Space held for 60 ticks leaves the title visible, but the original MENU resource identifies Command-N as New Game. That shortcut starts Level 1. After the ready delay, an equal-duration idle run moves Pac left to approximately x165; holding Right instead moves him right to approximately x279. Both runs end at frontend tick 1022 / guest tick 1622 with no exhausted frame budget, score 5 and three lives. The inspected maze captures establish bounded gameplay and directional input, not level completion, persistence or browser qualification. Actual PPC halts at PC zero, LR `010028A0`, immediately after `ISpInit`. Loader diagnostics identify unresolved weak `ISpGetGlobalElementList` with no transition vector; [#4382](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4382) tracks the missing import. The PPC route remains gameplay-unqualified.

The unchanged [PacMac Deluxe 1.3 archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/PacMac%20Deluxe%201.3.sit) is **1,441,512 bytes**, SHA-256 `28af1441f900e48508fed5747849d7cbb9e914a5334622179ec06fd78994cd74`, and expands 141 files including levels, music plugins, sprites, sounds, registration application, README and Software License. The README permits unchanged distribution with the documentation but requires permission for inclusion in a shareware collection on CD-ROM or other media. The Software License also prohibits commercial bundling without written permission and makes CD/floppy collection permission conditional. Applicability to this hosted catalogue and the separate music-plugin terms remain unverified. Actual 68K displays a fatal Sound Library initialization error with return code **-5** at frontend tick 180 and remains there at tick 780. No game was hosted and no gameplay screenshot was submitted.

## Monkey Shines trial click diagnosis

[#4381](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4381) tracks the unchanged 1.1.2 package after its merged file-ID startup fix. Fresh actual PPC runs at default depth and at 8-bit depth show a black or blank game surface after the Not Yet interaction. Command-N subsequently redraws the original trial notice; dismissal is not established. At 8-bit depth, tracing confirms `GlobalToLocal` correctly converts screen point **(443,250)** to **(310,59)** using the dialog origin **(133,191)**. The following guest `TrackControl` call nevertheless passes **(443,250)** to an enabled, visible control whose local bounds are **(300,14,320,104)**, producing part zero. The guest call site loads that point from its original event record. [Apple's TrackControl reference](https://leopard-adc.pepas.com/documentation/mac/Toolbox/Toolbox-332.html) specifies window-local coordinates. This is diagnostic evidence, not proof that the runtime should reinterpret the argument or bypass the trial. No functional tracking fix or registered-state change was made.

## Release 0.84.1 production verification

[Release PR #4376](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4376) merged through passing website/catalogue, MSRV, licence, package, Linux tests and both architecture showcases as `2aa2a7d2f1d1078f5f5d19b8a8305a3678cd98b8`. The [release workflow](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/38040018944) completed its website publishing job. The fully rendered [production Chiral page](https://systemless.org/chiral/) was inspected and reports **v0.84.1**, revision **2aa2a7d2f1d1**, while still explicitly stating that launching is not enabled. The original UDIF fix is therefore deployed; this release does not turn launch-disabled catalogue pages into qualified playable games.


## Pac the Man global input-list compatibility fix

The focused [#4382](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4382) fix resolves `ISpGetGlobalElementList`, validates its output buffer and exposes the existing keyboard plus three mouse elements through a stable opaque list. All **35 InputSprocket tests** pass, including new resolution/enumeration and invalid-output regressions; the native `ci-test` desktop build succeeds. The exact original archive now passes the former PPC startup halt and Command-N reaches Level 1. At frontend/guest tick **1022**, the held-Right capture places Pac at approximately global x360, while the equal-duration idle capture places him at approximately x245; both show score 5 and three lives with no exhausted frame budget. This establishes bounded PPC gameplay/input alongside the separate 68K evidence above. The PC-zero sound trace stops use the normal callback return sentinel and do not establish crashes. Callback frequency and audio fidelity still require verification in [#4383](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4383); sound fidelity, sustained play, persistence, hosting permission and browser approval remain unqualified. The unchanged archive is not staged for hosting on the strength of its freeware label alone.

## Oxyd expansion intake

The unchanged [Oxyd archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Oxyd.sit), linked from the [O index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/o/), is **685,917 bytes**, SHA-256 `e4022e27780425a730d03a5f7371a857bd9006b9654142c4a3b487e4ca2da6fd`. It expands 12 files: three executable variants, three data files, multilingual documentation, introductory notes and Finder metadata. The English README identifies **version 3.7** and documents Command-3/F3 for the first level. Inspected English, German and French data-fork documentation supplies use/installation instructions but no affirmative hosting grant; the remaining resource text still needs review. The separately indexed 3.8 installer endpoint returned HTTP 502; no complete 3.8 receipt is recorded.

An actual 68K/8-bit startup using selector `Oxyd/Oxyd 16 colors (8 Bit)` opens and reads the original Data A Color and Data B files. It then opens `.AOut` and `.AIn` as synthetic refs 131/132, but repeatedly calls PBRead on **65530 / signed -6**, which is not registered, receiving rfNumErr. The 180-tick first action did not reach a capture before the repetitive diagnostic was manually stopped; there is no completed play report or gameplay claim. Investigate the serial driver reference/status path before retesting. No archive was modified or staged for hosting.

## Pac the Man direct audio evidence

For [#4383](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4383), a direct `assert_audio_during` over 120 frontend ticks after the PPC matched-idle gameplay scenario fails with **zero non-silent samples**, at frontend/guest tick **1142**, with one audio assertion and zero exhausted frame budgets. Additional observational tracing shows SndStartFilePlay receives **ref=0, resource=0**, with no path, metadata or decoded samples, yet creates successful empty playback with an asynchronous completion. The original application has sound resource IDs 128–138, not ID 0. The resource route and missing-source errors need correction independently of normal callback sentinel returns. The original optional music README also requires placing the four songs beside the application; their unchanged archive currently stores them under `Pacmusic/`. No relocation, repacking or decoder change was used for these measurements.

### Missing-source fix validation

The #4383 fix treats file reference zero as the resource route even for ID zero, rejects absent/malformed resources and invalid or missing file data before playback, and preserves valid AIFF/resource playback. All **50 Sound Manager tests pass**, including a genuine resource ID zero, the valid AIFF fixture and error cases that create no channel, playback or completion callback. The rebuilt native runner replays the exact original PPC archive through frontend/guest tick **1022** with zero exhausted frame budgets. The inspected held-Right image still shows Level 1, score 5, three lives and Pac at approximately global x360. The separate 120-tick audio assertion still fails with **zero non-silent samples** at tick **1142**. This establishes the missing-source contract fix without claiming restored music, sustained gameplay or browser qualification.

## Chiral deployed-browser and player qualification

On 10 October 2026 the actual production page at `https://systemless.org/chiral/` loads deployed module `/systemless-org-b01da995bf849fb2.js`. Its exported browser benchmark fetches the unmodified Systemless object directly, with no request interception, mirror or modified CORS headers. An in-browser complete fetch returns **1,043,239 bytes**, SHA-256 **`b4c289ac4e8ee67836281d94ce54a9a724a49802106adb49a57741c5570b81d3`**. In GUI simulation mode the inspected checkpoints show the original trial notice, main menu, Level 1 board, and a purple atom placed on the board. The report completes at **guest tick 2100**, with zero assertions; this is inspected bounded gameplay evidence, not a frame-pacing, sound or level-completion claim. The replacement catalogue screenshot comes from that deployed browser benchmark and contains only the full game framebuffer.

A separate temporary player preview is built from runtime revision `49dfb08ce812837bf855f47de08eadd6af31a3d4` (the code subsequently merged in #4387). Only its temporary catalogue enables Chiral. Page and worker assets are served by an isolated local HTTPS preview at the production origin, using a test certificate and an owned browser DNS override; this is **preview evidence, not a deployed enabled route**. Requests to `assets.systemless.org` remain real and unmodified. The browser independently confirms HTTP 200 and the exact archive hash/size above. The player reports actual **68K** and **runtime worker=true**. Ordinary browser pointer input reaches Level 1 via small in-button drags; a subsequent board click places a purple atom and changes the dispenser to blue. The inspected final capture still contains that atom at guest tick **5580**, with tracking inactive.

Stationary clicks remain defective in #4373. Observational worker diagnostics confirm release was applied: at guest tick **4224**, `ranGuest=true`, `uiTracking=true`, and `canRelease=true`; the notice remains open. An eight-pixel drag inside Not Yet reaches the menu and ends tracking. This rules out merely withholding mouse-up in that reproduction, without identifying the root cause. A mixed-assets preview reload fell back to the main-thread runtime and is excluded from worker qualification. Tracked launch remains disabled; no registration code or archive modification is used. Sustained play, level completion, save/restart and final launch approval remain pending.


## Browser qualification — 10 October 2026

Tested release-mode browser runtime source `97ec1e637d085b447685cc055e4f0e9e8a862b65` (v0.84.1 plus the merged Pac input/sound fixes), compiled with `trunk build --release --locked`. Only the two catalogue launch flags were temporarily enabled for this preview; both source files were restored after the build. No runtime changes or guest patches.

The available local Chrome successfully opens localhost; this does not use the earlier extension-blocked cloud browser, alternate hostname, tunnel or disabled browser security. Normal DOM keyboard and pointer events reach the regular WebAssembly worker player. The canvas explicitly reports worker=true, architecture=68k, CPU pacing=8 MHz, max ticks per paint=2. For the local preview, CDP supplies independently fetched, hash-verified original archive bytes to the archive request, with local-preview CORS. This proves player interaction with exact bytes; it does not establish production CDN loading by itself.

Separately, ordinary HTTPS Chrome loaded the actual public systemless.org page and deployed `/systemless-org-b01da995bf849fb2.js`. The exported browser benchmark fetched the immutable Systemless-hosted archives from the real production origin without interception, alternate host mapping or security changes. Independent browser SHA-256 and byte counts match the catalogue. Inspected frames show iPuzzle scrambling and a tile move, and Kalaha Begin, a human extra turn and two AI replies. Both bounded scripts completed at guest tick 1520, with zero assertions; their passed flag is action completion, supplemented by inspected images, not a full-game compatibility verdict.

### iPuzzle
Normal release worker player: Command-T scrambles, two stationary legal tile clicks move adjacent tiles, and a nonadjacent click leaves the content unchanged pixel-for-pixel. Full puzzle solution and audible sound remain unverified. The original freeware distribution and ReadMe are unchanged.

### Kalaha
Normal release 68K worker player: Command-B fills six balls per pit; stationary clicks yield the human extra turn and AI replies. All captures are inspected. Original 30-day shareware state is preserved. Full match, all difficulties, saving and audio remain unverified.

Tracking: [iPuzzle #3944](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/3944), [Kalaha #3945](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/3945), [catalogue PR #3946](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/3946).

Kalaha’s normal PowerPC selector also launched a fresh release worker with `architecture=ppc`; Command-B, the human extra turn and two computer replies were inspected. The PPC board occupies a different content position, so clicks were placed from its actual capture. No guest patching or runtime changes were used.

Both pages displayed v0.85.0 and revision `20a88ca26533`, using `/systemless-org-bf29dd61f842aac7.js`. - [Kalaha](https://systemless.org/kalaha-11/): normal 68K and PPC workers at 8 MHz. Begin, human extra-turn moves and computer responses were inspected. The PowerPC selector starts the genuine PPC route; its canvas reports worker=true, architecture=ppc. Original archive: 398,473 bytes, SHA-256 `e30d98a0b2ecc8eeb50886d6ce5ba06335b1877a92746903f70dca45b6dd62bc`. Full match, all levels, saving and audio remain unverified.

These are two new distinct, rights-qualified live catalogue additions, rather than staged entries. Kalaha is an extra intake outside the primary 812 slots. The 192-record total and 172 launch flags still do not independently qualify the whole baseline.
