human-published v0.97.0 uses the actual immutable managed HQX, default 25 MHz,
68K/depth 8, Chrome 151 Worker/Canvas2D and 1000×900 host viewport. No production
bridge, local archive fixture, security override or release action. Original
About visibly shows UNREGISTERED. Not Yet followed by held Pheta menu → New Game
reaches complete Screen 1; held L visibly moves the player right. Inspected
before/after captures show walking. Two full-gameplay pacing probes pass the
unchanged minimum 50 at **53.906 / 51.856 guest ticks/sec**, without console
errors; a later longer-control probe also passes at 53.906.

The aligned sequence holds L for 1.5 seconds to reach the actual first ladder,
then A for two seconds to climb to the upper platform. Two fresh direct public
runs pass **eight fixed measured before/after climbing pixel checks each**, at
**52.824 / 51.787 guest ticks/sec**, with no console errors. Both actual captures
are inspected. Initial sprite-free paint captures are excluded from assertions;
only stable ladder-bottom and ladder-top captures qualify. Prior short walks
stopped adjacent to the ladder, while two-second L overshot it: those were
alignment probes, not evidence of a climbing defect.

Early Command-N was a harness mistake (actual visible shortcut is Command-G),
and its captures remained on the title screen. Title-screen samples fell below
the unchanged 50 gate (42.722 / 42.713); preserve those failures separately from
successful Screen 1 samples. Actual managed HQX GET independently matches
281,913 bytes and the original SHA-256. The original conditional rights,
fork/Finder parity, native walking/climbing, promotion and launch approval are
recorded in [#4628](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4628).
Entry [#4629](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4629) is merged as
`1463e21ee247d07ac223ba205df3fb50b5a25585`. Human v0.97.0
[release pipeline](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/38246494927)
passed website deployment, six native builds and binary upload. This is the
**nineteenth independently qualified new live game**, one distinct original
68K title; no native PPC slice. Full levels, jumping, saving, audio and the known
Options/help/post-Options rendering faults remain unverified or unresolved.
Intake stays open for those faults. No release action taken.

preservation checks**) at **1,748 frontend / 2,326 guest ticks**, zero exhaustion.

regions match exactly while human positions differ. Captures inspected. This is

match/scoring/save/audio qualification.

## Brick Attack 1.0 original PowerPC intake

Target 107, rank 295. [Original archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Brick%20Attack%201.0.sit): **1,244,481 bytes**, SHA-256 `abc9aea8dc3b7356abfe755dc27e0e293becd34b6bb82567bc5c1e0f7905d396`. Independent extraction and production packing agree on all five files, ten forks and supported Finder metadata. The game has a PowerPC PEF and cfrg, with no CODE resources; the separate registration helper does not establish a 68K game slice.

The original readme permits complete unchanged nonprofit redistribution, retains the five-level unregistered limit and prohibits game disassembly. Apple DrawSprocketLib 1.1.2 is included; its distribution scope remains unverified. Preserve the entire archive and original restrictions. No hosting clearance, registration bypass, game modification or author contact is claimed.

[Compatibility defect #4762](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4762): the indexed-only PixPat decoder fell back to unrelated monochrome bytes for the original 16/32-bit colour patterns, producing white stripes. The proposed direct-colour decoder renders the original texture: **84,600 background pixels independently match original ppat 1000** after RGB555 conversion. The older blank black background is not an independent oracle. Existing gameplay checks now use original-resource background values while retaining ball-flight and struck-brick assertions. The candidate passes **11 assertions**, at **1,725 frontend/guest ticks**, zero exhaustion; final captures show a launched ball, a removed red brick and score 10. **134 native QuickDraw tests pass**, including padded-row, tiling, xRGB padding and indexed/16-bit destination checks.

Tested native SHA-256 `820508c7a36cd1d5576802bde0d13cf34abb36ccefc10ee3d19577db288b9be6`; explicit PowerPC, literal arrows, default 640×480/16-bit display. This is bounded gameplay and original-resource rendering evidence, not complete game qualification. Paddle-control causality, browser pacing, bundled-library rights, catalogue CI/promotion/launch approval and public qualification remain pending in [intake #4689](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4689). No live increment or release action.

## Genuine pinball demo intakes: Lost Continent and Tristan

### Lost Continent original PPC demo — primary 111

[Original download](https://classicmacdemos.com/download/3d-ultra-pinball-the-lost-continent/): **6,449,029 bytes**, SHA-256 `c16d1d2c10fa2121ba6ecc51d57c96582cc2345a6ec20448f864795462595a2c`. Independent extraction exposes four files: Pinball 3 Demo, Demo Data, Demo Map, v0. All **four files/eight forks and supported Finder type/creator/flags independently match production packing**, with exhaustive inventory coverage; the private production pack is 10,575,713 bytes. The v0 PEF exports InitTable; it is a table module, not a replacement C++ runtime. Main executable is PowerPC PEF; resource fork contains cfrg and two CODE resources. Original strings explicitly require Power Macintosh. Auxiliary CODE resources do not establish a playable 68K edition.

Initial native PPC startup halted at InterfaceLib:PixMap32Bit. [Query fix #4768](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4768) merged after all nine checks passed. This is an addressing-mode query, not pixel depth; [Apple QD01](https://leopard-adc.pepas.com/technotes/qd/qd_01.html) documents pmVersion 4. The original colour prompt then exposed missing SaveEntries. [Palette fix #4770](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/4770) merged with all nine checks passing; its two focused owned-handle/aliasing tests and 135-test QuickDraw suite pass. The remaining blank frame was isolated to copied bitmap proxies being read as direct PixMaps (bogus depth 787). The proxy candidate resolves both pixels and colour tables through the real handle, passes all **137 native QuickDraw tests**, and renders the original Sierra/Dynamix splash and five-minute-demo introduction. Eight measured introduction assertions pass at **1,738 frontend/guest ticks**, zero exhaustion. Ordinary continuation then halts at `MSL RuntimePPC++.DLL:__register_fragment`; this remains a separate startup blocker, not gameplay qualification. Original restrictions and archive remain unchanged. Tested proxy native SHA-256 `3e82b39ce4c7dff452ac8f773be3572c913c904c684d738fdd44bf399f75099f`. [Intake #4764](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4764). No standalone readme in the package; affirmative redistribution permission remains pending. No gameplay, hosting or publication qualification.

### Genuine Tristan pinball demo — primary 115

[Original download](https://classicmacdemos.com/download/tristan/): **657,581 bytes**, SHA-256 `278d01a636388a888b76285ebcf27d2a8dcb7e6548e486e7393993eb9704b602`. This genuine LittleWing pinball archive supersedes the unrelated Trist board-game lead. Independent extraction exposes Tristan™ Demo, External Sound, screen1. All **three files/six forks and supported Finder type/creator/flags independently match production packing**, with exhaustive inventory coverage; the private production pack is 1,227,394 bytes. Game data fork is empty; 751,039-byte resource fork has five CODE resources and no cfrg: 68K only. Original requirements include 68020/FPU, System 6.0.6+, Color QuickDraw and 256 colours.

Native 68K/depth 8 shows the original title at 600 frontend/1,200 guest ticks, zero exhaustion. Ordinary title click and 600 further frontend ticks show “Application program damaged. Please copy from master disk and try again.” Captures inspected; the cause is unverified, so do not label the archive corrupt or bypass the alert. Zero-assertion diagnostics do not qualify gameplay. [Intake #4765](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4765). No standalone readme; affirmative hosting grant remains pending. Production decoding matches every independently extracted byte, so the damage alert cannot be attributed to a decoder fork mismatch.

Both diagnostics used native SHA-256 `820508c7a36cd1d5576802bde0d13cf34abb36ccefc10ee3d19577db288b9be6`. Originals unchanged; no game patches, registration bypasses, managed uploads, live increments or release actions.

## Susan 2.5 — additional reserve discovery

[Author's original page](https://slinhart.com/sue/suemac.html); [current author archive](https://slinhart.com/archive.html); [original complete archive](https://slinhart.com/sue/SUSAN_Pack.sit): **472,838 bytes**, SHA-256 `cc62394e6c6cc8bbedf8c90bd998e7bcd6fcb73c57926dbedec59cb9fffda659`.

The bundled Pack Read Me explicitly encourages free redistribution of the complete archive with every file included, forbids modifications to the program and associated files, and permits charges only for shipping, handling and media. ## Friends of Nim 1.0 — primary 732

[Original index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/n/); [binary archive](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Nim%201.0%20Folder.sit): **94,846 bytes**, SHA-256 `68126dbaaee3dffbbadd12be50db9ea8c5fb684d9d7cc35d1cf0e53c9c38dc55`. Original Friends of Nim application has empty data fork, 163,477-byte resource fork, six CODE resources and no cfrg: **68K only**. All **two files/four forks and supported Finder metadata match independent extraction and production packing**, exhaustive inventory; KPK2 is 181,577 bytes. This collection counts as one title, not nine variant games.

Original application TEXT 640 explicitly identifies copyright 1995 Mark Pilgrim and GNU GPL version 2 or later. The complete binary package includes GNU General Public License. [Author-submitted historical source archive](https://www.nic.funet.fi/pub/mac/info-mac/_Development/_Source_Code/friends-of-nim-c.hqx): **392,947 bytes**, SHA-256 `b6f8386f03acd6d3f01d26b1d3294cc3911b0cbeb4cc827df5d72d24e470a1b4`; original mail header identifies Friends of Nim 1.0 source code, 9 July 1995. Independent extraction yields 110 files including C/header sources, project, original resource files, licence and compiled game. All **155 compiled-game resource payloads, names and attributes match** the binary distribution, including every CODE resource. Whole resource forks differ at six reserved-header bytes; do not claim identical whole-fork hashes.

Original Legal talk credits Jim's CDEFs. Actual binary components are **CDEF 101: 7,084 bytes; CDEF 102: 6,170 bytes**, also present in the original source package's nim dialogs.rsrc. [Jim Stout's original library 1.50](https://www.nic.funet.fi/pub/mac/info-mac/_Development/_Library/jims-cdefs-15.hqx): **1,459,967 bytes**, SHA-256 `18f0dc49d19ce6f6309685d8bbb74153c2ad48d273c67d18d7476b0b99f6177c`. Its original Conditions for use permit use in Macintosh products and modified versions, retain copyright and require redistributed library/source to preserve the entire original distribution. Contact, credit and donations are expressly optional; no contact or approval request made. All 58 included binary control payloads were compared, and **none exactly matches Nim's two components**. [Author's earlier 1.20 submission](https://info-mac.org/viewtopic.php?t=14262) identifies popup 101/spinner 102 and an earlier archive lead; matching control source/distribution review remains pending. Preserve both original GPL and control credits. No affirmative full-package hosting clearance claimed yet.

Native first-run Return and ordinary Command-N originally draw empty columns: source calls GetIconSuite with small-family selector then PlotIconSuite for each 16x16 tile, but classic Icon Utilities selector 0x0306 returned paramErr. Candidate loaded-suite drawing now selects 32x32, 16x16 and 16x12 families and deepest usable 1/4/8-bit data; uses original mandatory masks, aligns mask ink, respects current port regions, maps indexed and direct destinations, and preserves Pascal OSErr/frame/registers. Seven neutral tests and **all 772 QuickDraw tests pass**. Nil/invalid suites and missing masks retain errors. Picture accumulation, labels, open/offline transforms, icon caches/unloaded resources and native PPC bindings remain unsupported or unverified; they are not silently reported successful.

Immutable candidate native SHA-256 `533eeadeaa2c8c96d9fca9c9d08a7ef3a040d2da0c24a12b70077bbebb2ee553`, exact executable `Nim 1.0 Folder/Friends of Nim`, default display and 68K: original normal Nim now draws columns **7,8,9,3,3,9,4** (43 stones). Ordinary bottom-stone click shows the original red selection; a second click within DoubleTime removes one. Computer removes six from column two, leaving **6,2,9,3,3,9,4** (36), XOR zero, matching its original source strategy. Fresh unchanged replay passes **twelve measured removal/retention checks at 1,419 frontend / 2,019 guest ticks**, zero exhausted frames; actual captures inspected. This is bounded normal-Nim gameplay, not a full-game, misere, all-variants, audio/save or ROM-fidelity claim. No browser/promotion/entry/public qualification or count increment. [Intake #4777](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4777); [runtime issue #4778](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4778).

Independent extraction yields 24 distinct files (35 archive inventory records include duplicate fork records), including the original README, colour and sound resources, movies and preset boards. The game data fork is a 210,778-byte PowerPC PEF executable; its 582,255-byte resource fork includes `cfrg` and substantial classic `CODE` resources. Native startup selects both 68K and PPC. The production packer produces a 3,738,748-byte KPK2 containing all 24 distinct paths. Independent comparison confirms all 48 data/resource forks and supported Finder metadata match the original extraction exactly; strict end-of-container and complete path-set checks pass.

The README explicitly describes an online demo and distinguishes the larger commercial CD-ROM version. It documents “Play Demo Game” / Command-N, initial piece placement and three actions per turn. No affirmative redistribution grant was found in the inspected README or game notice resources. Demo availability alone does not qualify hosting; no catalogue entry or asset promotion is claimed.

Using unchanged archive contents, executable selector `AmoebArena™ Demo/AmoebArena™ Demo`, and native binary SHA-256 `533eeadeaa2c8c96d9fca9c9d08a7ef3a040d2da0c24a12b70077bbebb2ee553` (the tested #4782 candidate), the 68K path reaches the illustrated demo title after ordinary clicks and then the player setup dialog through Command-N. The bounded setup probe completed 1,518 frontend ticks / 2,118 guest ticks with no instruction-budget exhaustion. Accepting the original setup with an ordinary held click then reaches the empty placement board, with both active player portraits and the 6 / 5 / 1 / 6 piece-count strip visible. This follow-up completes 1,824 frontend ticks / 2,424 guest ticks with no instruction-budget exhaustion. The startup probes have zero assertions. A subsequent ordinary click places a red cell and reduces the first inventory count from six to five. A fresh bounded replay passes one measured pixel assertion for that first placement and finishes with three visibly occupied cells and the first count at three (2,370 frontend / 2,970 guest ticks, zero budget exhaustion). Six clicks were attempted, but only three placements are evidenced; the script checkpoint name is not a placement count. No completed setup, turn, computer reply or gameplay qualification is claimed.

The PPC path displays an alert that the PPC Toolbox is absent. Original PEF import metadata includes `InterfaceLib:PPCInit`; the shared Gestalt implementation advertises the `ppc ` selector only for 68K, and no native PPCInit binding was located. This is a concrete API/capability investigation, not evidence that changing the capability bit alone would provide working communication services. No demo patch, unlock or successful stub was applied. Browser gameplay, screenshots for publication, rights qualification and public deployment remain pending.

### AstroRock initialization error handling

The generic NetSprocket initialization adapter returns Apple SDK `kNSpOTNotPresentErr` (-30370), because no native Open Transport provider exists. It creates no initialized networking instance and reports no networking success. This allows the original application to handle the failure rather than halting at an unresolved import. Original Apple Universal Interfaces `NetSprocket.h` / `MacErrors.h`, preserved at [pinned SDK source](https://github.com/tomasen/splayer/blob/fb3853a81bb5235d6024456e1d239d2b35cc675c/include/qt/MacErrors.h), establish the C OSStatus ABI and error constant; [Apple's reference](https://leopard-adc.pepas.com/documentation/Carbon/Reference/Games_Sprockets_Legacy/gamesprock_legacy_ref.pdf) documents that networking initialization may fail.

All forty native Toolbox utility regressions pass, including default and explicit initialization arguments, repeated failure, preservation of non-result arguments/stack and exact library binding. Fresh immutable native binary SHA-256 `08fd3ae97fb442ef22a5805eec5a1572db2920ea31e55f356bd25aedd9f45732`, source `0706b3e78b9b8b381b5c79a4875442aaaff5ffc9`, unchanged original archive, exact packed selector `Astrorock 2000 Demo/Astrorock 2000™ Demo`, explicit PPC: the original title screen now appears at 600 frontend/guest ticks with zero exhaustion. Capture inspected. This zero-assertion startup replay does not establish gameplay, networking, browser or hosting qualification. Preserve the earlier failed startup and unresolved rights evidence. Actual transport/networking support remains open in #3967.

### Candy Crisis weak MPYield and the next library boundary

Original publisher `main.c` waits for TickCount to advance inside QuickFadeIn and calls MPYield in that loop. Independent PEF metadata identifies **CarbonLib:MPYield, import 167, weak transition-vector class/flags 0x82**. The failed trace returns original dialog item 3, enters DrawSprocket and draws original picture 300 before the zero-address call. Apple Multiprocessing.h declares **void MPYield(void)**. The candidate resolves this real import, completes its void return preserving general registers, and yields the current execution slice; resumption does not re-enter the call. This is not a multiprocessing worker-task implementation.

The weak relocation/procedure/yield/resumption regression passes, as do all **23 native Thread Manager tests**. Native binary SHA-256 `8a02bf30e7ca4f36178e40def6da52624956a65060bfbe9a8e4c5b20320c0ceb`, unchanged archive/exact game selector, explicit PPC, ordinary Not Yet click: the initial frontend-601 failure is cleared. The original fade finishes and the game enters its bundled shared library, then **fails at frontend 626 / guest 625**, PC **0**, SP `0x07F6FCC0`, LR `0x0300304C`, zero exhaustion/assertions. Preserve this failed continuation. Recent imports are SndSoundManagerVersion, Gestalt and GetSoundOutputInfo; no particular next missing call is yet proved. Original libmikmod metadata includes weak NewSndCallBackUPP/DisposeSndCallBackUPP, with no native bindings found, providing the next concrete investigation. [Continuation issue #4792](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4792) stays open. No gameplay/browser/rights qualification or new entry is claimed.


### Candy Crisis sound callback wrapper progress

Apple Sound.h defines NewSndCallBackUPP as creation of a current-architecture routine descriptor with Pascal void/two-pointer procedure info **0x000003C0**; DisposeSndCallBackUPP delegates to DisposeRoutineDescriptor. The native candidate binds the original weak APIs and uses those existing owned descriptor services. The focused neutral test passes, and all **67 Mixed Mode + 45 Sound Manager + 24 interrupt-callback tests pass** (136 total, including the new test).

Immutable native SHA-256 `021be1959e166b1550d389478a740a6cce16c8c1c1bc523ac1c33d9f40c305f8`, unchanged original archive/exact selector, PPC: the previous shared-library initialization failure is cleared. Trace shows the game creating its temporary module file, the library issuing SndDoImmediate/SndDoCommand, and the main game loading sound resources and pictures. The bounded continuation still **fails at frontend 630 / guest 629**, zero exhausted frames/assertions. An earlier nested sound callback returns normally through the runtime's **PC-zero / LR-zero sentinel**, with library TOC `0x0303A060`; this is not a callback failure. Detailed load/sound tracing identifies the later blocker: **sound completion callback CycleLimit { cycles: 250000 } at PC `0x03025D54`, UPP `0x07D50000`, no unsupported import**. The runner marks that unfinished callback as a guest halt. Failure capture is blank and inspected. Callback work needs retained budget/resumption handling; #4792 remains open. The report's zero exhausted-frame counter does not rule out this separate callback limit. Do not infer a new unsupported API or claim gameplay/audio/browser qualification from this partial progress.


### Candy Crisis native sound callback slice resumption

Sound callback PR #4797 merged at `1fc6adf3305a552ce66b9a72f874ce49341dce7d` after all nine checks passed, including the corrected runaway watchdog regression. No release was triggered.

[Callback budget issue #4796](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4796). Candidate retains unfinished native sound completion/doubleback callbacks in the existing parked-callback lifecycle. A native-slice wait has no fabricated 68K call ID; callback registers and command/frame storage remain installed, interrupt delivery stays masked, and execution resumes before restoring the interrupted context at the real callback return. Existing ownership/Mixed Mode refusal guards remain. Unsupported imports and non-budget faults retain failure handling; no callback is skipped or reported complete at a slice boundary, and the 250,000-cycle callback cap is unchanged.

The finite multi-slice neutral callback proves retained command bytes, masking, retirement and exact architectural caller restoration. Its fixture starts the native engine and binds the application PowerPC task. All **25 interrupt-callback + 28 Mixed Mode runner + 12 audio regressions pass** (65, including the new test). The existing 45 Sound Manager tests also passed with the same runtime implementation. Native build succeeds.

CI then exposed the existing runaway doubleback regression: an unconditional PowerPC `b .` must remain fatal at the watchdog. The corrected candidate refuses to park that terminal self-branch while retaining finite callbacks across slices. Local verification of this correction passes the focused runaway test, all 27 tests matching interrupt callbacks (including finite resumption), and all 18 runner execution tests. These sets overlap; they are not an additional distinct-test total. The native game capture below predates this watchdog correction; updated CI remains the merge gate.

Immutable native SHA-256 `32beaaf1d9d46625cb9b29e3357181108b102fd5e99ce941bc7f38735fc611e7`, unchanged original archive/exact selector, PPC: ordinary held **Not Yet** click now completes **1,206 frontend/guest ticks**, zero exhausted frames and zero assertions. Trace has 408 original library SndDoCommand callback queue operations; no audio waveform fidelity claim follows from that count. Actual final capture is inspected: main menu with UNREGISTERED and animated candy elements, but substantial missing/placeholder image and font/menu rendering, including the Photo-JPEG decompressor fallback message. This fixes the execution-slice halt and proves bounded menu progression; gameplay, controls/scoring, browser pacing and complete audio/graphics qualification remain pending. Preserve previous failed runs. No new entry or qualified-live count increment.

### Candy Crisis compressed PICT artwork

[Rendering issue #4798](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/4798): candidate decodes original QuickTime PICT `jpeg` and `png ` payloads through the existing image dependency, with declared-dimension/data checks and decoder limits. Source crop and destination clipping remain active; indexed, RGB555 and RGB32 colour conversion is covered. A successful decode suppresses the original QuickDraw warning; damaged data preserves it. All **61 PICT tests pass**, including five new decoder/rendering tests. Native build passes.

## Myth: The Fallen Lords — native qualification after directory and write repairs

Rechecked 27 October 2026 (Sydney). Primary target 10 uses the original [Classic Mac Demos installer](https://classicmacdemos.com/download/myth-i-the-fallen-lords/): **32,598,957 bytes**, SHA-256 `00883533c8990d936dd5381ce1566ec6f783e9b98353202dbf3e0ae119e03c98`. The unchanged archive selects a native PPC application. The 68K installer wrapper does not establish a 68K game slice. Previously inspected bundled READMEs still do not establish archive redistribution permission; no hosting or entry is authorised by this runtime evidence.

Released v0.100.0 reproduces the missing `tags.gor` alert. A File Manager trace finds both data files incorrectly under `modules`, while the application correctly asks for `tags`. [Directory repair #5063](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5063), merged as `63d057d7016ee6251a80bc0d983a81d78a9e9d01`, interprets the full VISE 3.6 catalog parent fields as nesting depth. Fourteen parser tests pass; the neutral nested/sibling-folder regression fails on the previous parser. Both files now open under `tags`. The distinct [Vintage Apple Mac installer](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Myth%20Demo%20Installer.sit), 32,596,349 bytes, SHA-256 `58cd142482b049c6432004e98a1a86f5ce36a90f53b555cd0af46d8beb983152`, also parses with the corrected paths; its runtime is not qualified by the primary archive's results.

The directory-fix candidate reaches the resolution dialog, menu and Training Map. A fresh tutorial replay passes eight measured W-forward/Z-left camera checks at 2,589 frontend/guest ticks, zero exhausted frames. Initial flag clicks did not demonstrate troop commands. Crow's Bridge subsequently returns to the menu in that candidate; retain this failed qualification rather than treating the tutorial as completed combat.

Tracing reveals a separate [PBWrite defect #5067](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/5067): rewriting a header truncates the entire file. The recording shrinks from 1,892 to 64 bytes, and reading its 1,828-byte body returns eofErr; sound preferences similarly lose eight bytes. [Repair #5069](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5069), merged as `0f57713398564e50b2abb73e13739a48e22667b2` from tested source `db4e16fb9d35ea8e26ade8bb7dde1cd81903676b`, preserves the unwritten suffix and grows the file only when required. Its import-level regression fails before the repair; all **201 File Manager tests pass** after it, including header/middle overwrites, zero-byte writes before EOF, sparse extension and resulting file positions.

The integrated native source is `8ad8e5c7c59543f7a25ec6e07e958a726ea44879` (merged directory repair plus the then-candidate PBWrite repair, subsequently merged in #5069). Optimized executable SHA-256: `3565753594974e477542b1ab01186d8ddff64163b552426a5db72193f47a6db2`. With the exact unchanged primary archive, `--headless --prefer-powerpc`, default display and startup time, the recording retains all 1,892 bytes and its body reads successfully; sound preferences retain 72 bytes and their eight-byte body reads successfully. The inspected sequence reaches Crow's Bridge's rendered battlefield at 3,072 frontend/guest ticks, zero exhaustion. The earlier gameplay comparison also includes intervening master changes; the focused red/green regression independently isolates the write repair's effect on file contents.

An ordinary held click selects **Thayer (Warrior)** and another ground click commands movement toward the bridge. A fresh repeat passes **eight measured selection/movement checks at 3,278 frontend/guest ticks**, zero exhaustion. In a matched run without the destination click, the selected warrior continues toward the lower edge instead. Both captures were inspected: the control is not stationary, but its different path supports player-controlled movement. This is bounded native mission entry and troop movement, not victory, full combat, save/reload, audio, multiplayer or independent ROM-fidelity qualification.

Reproduction scripts, reports, captures and logs are retained locally under `/Users/ben/.cache/systemless-catalogue-1000/myth/`, particularly `combat-verified.json`, `combat-verified/report.json`, `combat-control/` and `integrated-battle.log`. No game binaries or play-script collection are committed. Rights clearance, production packing parity, a hosted archive, browser input/pacing, asset promotion, entry approval and public qualification remain pending. Record and qualified-live counts are unchanged.


## Barrack 1.0.4 — native menu and barrier qualification

The exact original archive from the [preserved distribution](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Barrack%201.0.4.sit) remains unchanged: **3,081,805 bytes**, SHA-256 `83e99f16e5d3f368247dbc31c8db8ccbe4ea63e0ab854f0a1a16da83e1c366f3`. Its bundled licence permits complete unchanged nonprofit distribution; preserve the original 30-day trial and registration program. The bundled FAQ identifies Greg Lovette as author and March 1996 as the initial release. No for-profit permission is inferred. [Catalogue intake #5080](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/5080) stages the complete original installer, not a stripped or rebuilt game archive.

Three distinct native PPC defects were isolated. [Streamed picture repair #5072](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5072), merged as `9950364dfe0c40dfd9787deb8e9d46590cb28fa8`, executes the original getPicProc callbacks without replacing the ten-byte picture header. [Store-update repair #5077](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5077) handles the original `sthu r5,0x082c(0)` according to classic processor behavior; the pre-existing core halt was reproduced both before and after the picture repair. [Port clipping repair #5078](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5078) preserves the game window when the background port draws its picture: the original 44-byte visible region excludes x=79..721, y=59..541, but the old picture path ignored it. The store-update repair merged as `c7acedfed359e962e9691a1633c9d3cb6c29fff7`; the clipping repair remains a candidate at this evidence revision. Store tests and the native exception suite pass (fourteen distinct tests); all 141 PPC QuickDraw tests and all 60 shared PICT tests pass with clipping.

Clean integrated source **`d4e39b207e0ab8c6d02478c124f17e0047dd11b8`**, optimized executable SHA-256 **`e02385344b48e7951d1cb046acc5eeb5f84c24a1103db37ebcad89e679c268da`**, exact selector `Barrack 1.0.4/Barrack 1.0.4 ƒ/Barrack`, `--headless --prefer-powerpc`, default display and fixed default startup time: the trial notice remains intact, its ordinary **Not Yet** dismissal retains the complete menu, and **Play Barrack** enters the game. Moving the mouse relocates the barrier gun. Firing at h=320/v=500 creates a horizontal barrier, clears **9%** and scores **90**. The side-panel values are labelled underneath: this is level **1** with **8** lives, not level 8.

A fresh replay passes **eight measured pixel assertions and one non-silent audio assertion** at **1,632 frontend / 1,862 guest ticks**, zero exhausted frames. A matched run with the firing click omitted passes its separate black-field check and stays at **0% / score 0**; both captures were inspected. Scripts and reports remain outside tracked source under `/Users/ben/.cache/systemless-catalogue-1000/barrack/`, particularly `barrier-verified.json`, `ppc-barrier-verified/`, `barrier-control.json` and `ppc-barrier-control/`. This supports bounded movement and scored barrier play, not complete levels, sustained play, settings persistence, sound fidelity or independent ROM equivalence.

The staged catalogue route offers PPC only and keeps launch disabled. The original 68K slice still has incomplete picture streaming (#4403). Independent inner-installer fork parity, managed asset verification, browser input/pacing approval and released public qualification remain pending. A current browser inventory has no connected browser, and an attempted in-app tab returns “Browser is not available: iab.” Keep the staged-record count separate from qualified-live delivery.


### Barrack managed-download source reconciliation

The first catalogue promotion attempt ([run 40346561689](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/40346561689)) fails before upload because Vintage Apple Mac returns HTTP 403 to GitHub CI. It is a distinct intact archive, not claimed byte-identical to the initial source.

The outer installer name differs. Production expansion preserves all nineteen records with identical relative paths, both forks and supported Finder metadata after stripping that outer prefix; neither duplicate Documentation Icon record is dropped. This verifies cross-source equivalence, not independent inner VISE installation fidelity.

A fresh replay of the exact Tucows archive uses selector **`Barrack Installer/Barrack 1.0.4 ƒ/Barrack`** and the same clean `d4e39b207` candidate executable. It passes the nine pixel/audio checks plus all **six exact full-frame comparisons** to the already inspected initial-source replay: **fifteen assertions**, 1,632 frontend / 1,862 guest ticks, zero exhausted frames. The final actual capture was inspected. Keep this a determinism/source-equivalence comparison, not an independent image oracle. The staged entry now pins the Tucows archive and adjusted selector; the unchanged screenshot depicts the same verified frame. Managed promotion, browser approval and released public qualification remain pending.


The fresh Tucows no-fire control also passes its one field assertion and all six exact full-frame comparisons at the same 1,632 / 1,862 clocks, zero exhaustion (`ppc-tucows-control/`). [Managed promotion 40348054188](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/40348054188) succeeds and commits immutable sources as `85acf8260`. Fresh public `curl` GETs of the managed archive and screenshot match the pinned **3,081,907-byte / `1d02dfb5…677974`** archive and **552,945-byte / `21e7e2a8…b21514`** screenshot. Local production validation passes for **220 staged records**. Browser qualification remains unavailable and launch remains disabled; neither asset promotion nor these HTTP checks constitute browser gameplay or a qualified-live increment.


## Barrack 1.0.4 — native gameplay and dialog repairs, 27 October 2026

This subsequent evidence supersedes the unresolved 68K startup and black PPC playfield observations above. The original trial notice is dismissed through **Not Yet**; no registration bypass is used.

### Merged compatibility repairs

- [#5087](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5087) resumes classic streamed-picture callbacks cooperatively and restores the 68K menu. The upstream return-link repair is a strong match, but the version update contains other changes, so this is not a single-commit causal isolation.

Setup now displays Sound volume, Music volume, both Low values, and the complete three-line small-text keybinding block. All 74 PPC Dialog Manager tests and four Standard File checks pass; six measured native setup assertions pass at 1,326 frontend / 1,556 guest ticks, zero exhausted frames. Captures were inspected.

The longer PPC replay first reaches 52% cleared using horizontal barriers, with collisions and life loss. Space changes the gun orientation; two vertical shots raise the cleared area to 73% and 78%. ## Hextris 1.0 — reserve R50 identity

The [Vintage Apple Mac H index](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/software/games/h/) links [Hextris Installer.sit](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Hextris%20Installer.sit). The unchanged download is **1,663,680 bytes**, SHA-256 **`2d6bf65ffbb528a881f6beec3bb73a5f49bb758c51d0e289186f23209fd25ad3`**. Independent `unar` inspection exposes a MindVision installer with a 1,550,841-byte data fork and 207,989-byte resource fork. Its embedded readme identifies **Mark S. Andersson / Hellosh Software, version 1.0, February 1998**. This is not David Markley's earlier MacHextris described at hextris.com; that author's history does not establish this archive's identity or permission.

The bundled requirements specify System 7.5 or later, a 256-color 640-by-480 display, Sound Manager 3.0 and QuickTime 2.0 for music. Production expansion yields 18 records, including the game, instructions, music, registration application and distinct duplicate-named icon resources. The selector is `Hextris Installer/Hextris 1.0 ƒ/Hextris`. Its 334,830-byte data fork (SHA-256 `7a4b93d2bb45cf063b231044396e7149fe9513b6d882ab3c89d0a55bd3c0892f`) contains a PowerPC PEF; the 2,560,497-byte resource fork (SHA-256 `b313a1377e7aafc3d0a7ece62c3b3754de7aa1cbddbb3c71d5e2952f60834f07`) includes a PowerPC `cfrg` member and 68K `CODE` 0/1 resources. This establishes both CPU slices; production expansion does not establish independent inner-installer parity.

The installed Read Me's Copyright Notice and license permit nonprofit redistribution of the unchanged, complete original installation package without prior written notice. For-profit distribution requires written permission, and individual resources, pictures, sounds or music cannot be separately redistributed. The original 30-day trial and registration requirement remain intact; unregistered play is limited to level 3. These terms do not authorize removing the trial or distributing an extracted executable alone.

Bounded native evidence uses immutable executable SHA-256 `8c6b9eb81faf7e14d16ea3cc82cbd52f9addf4c3818f12611750bfc57a165f6e`, source commit `b3c50cee67bd22d10576249b16b07e75076e1e2c`, and the exact original archive above:

- **68K:** dismissing the original trial dialog and starting with Command-B reaches play. J movement, I rotation and Space slam produce score 100, two movement counts, one rotation and one slam, with a placed piece visible. The same-timing control omits only these gameplay inputs and remains at score/movement/rotation/slam zero. Fresh input and control replays each pass five measured pixel assertions at 2,330 frontend ticks / 2,930 guest ticks, with zero exhausted frames. Actual started-game, input-final and control-final screenshots were inspected. These are bounded input checks, not a full level, sound or full-game qualification.
- **PowerPC:** startup stops before the first frontend tick completes at unsupported `InterfaceLib:InitPalettes`, PC `01F000BC`; tracked in [#5106](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/5106). No PPC gameplay is claimed.

The 68K trial dialog still lacks expected labels/artwork. Independent inner-installer comparison, remaining compatibility work, full qualification, accurate catalogue capture, managed asset promotion and browser/public-route verification remain outstanding. No asset was promoted and no catalogue entry was added.

Original archive and local inspection files remain under `/Users/ben/.cache/systemless-catalogue-1000/hextris/`. R50 now fills primary slot 813 to replace the confirmed Checkers Deluxe duplicate; it does not increment qualified-live progress.


### Hextris 1.0 — repaired native verification and staged entry

The combined candidate at source `c67b094c303187f464a58be2a00b967af366b310`, executable SHA-256 `06c2bde97f27e9e787a52fccfeb69161bb4db19eb968093e9cb7c51d82e1a308`, retests the same unchanged installer after [PPC palette initialization #5109](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5109) (merged), [classic palette initialization #5114](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5114), and [application-control dialog drawing #5117](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/pull/5117).

The 68K registration dialog now visibly restores the logo, trial text, name/code labels and second field. Five measured content checks pass at 1,200 frontend / 1,800 guest ticks. Its existing gameplay and matched-control scripts each still pass five checks at 2,330 / 2,930 ticks. PPC now passes the prior startup halt: accept the normal eight-bit display prompt, dismiss the original trial dialog with Not Yet, and start with Command-B. J movement, I rotation and Space slam reach score 100 with movement 2, rotation 1 and slam 1. Fresh PPC input and matched-control replays each pass five measured checks at 2,933 frontend / guest ticks; the control remains at zero. All these runs have zero exhausted frames, and actual captures were inspected. No level-completion or audio claim is made.

The apparent PPC border corruption was investigated rather than patched. `DrawBorder` randomly selects one of fourteen styles; a scoped register trace records style 2 at game start, and the original PICT 10002 contains its gray/beige speckled artwork. The traced final image is byte-identical to the earlier probe. [Investigation #5118](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/5118) is closed as not reproduced; matching random appearances across CPUs is not a correctness criterion or an independent oracle.

[The Hextris entry](catalogue/hextris.md) stages both architectures with launch disabled and a gameplay capture cropped without scaling to the 68K game content surface. [Intake #5121](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/issues/5121) keeps independent inner-VISE extraction comparison, remaining qualification, managed asset promotion, browser approval and the human-released public route open. This proposed record brings source stock to 220, not qualified-live stock. The intact original package and its original trial terms remain authoritative.


### Hextris source download follow-up

The initial URL returned HTTP 403 during [managed promotion](https://github.com/benletchford/systemless/actions/runs/40390463566). The index also offers [Hextris Installer.sit.sit](https://www.vintageapplemac.com/files/games/Hextris%20Installer.sit.sit), downloaded unchanged as **1,661,103 bytes**, SHA-256 **`db29b2e7753f0eda79146525c15445953d0780f3f93a87aa7a2a646ef4190fe3`**. This is the new integrity-pinned source; promotion remains subject to CI access and hosted-byte verification.

Independent `unar` extraction exposes a StuffIt wrapper containing a 1,660,884-byte StuffIt archive and then the original VISE installer. Its 1,550,841-byte data fork matches the first source exactly. The 207,989-byte resource fork differs at only 24 positions within bytes 70–95, before its resource data starts at offset 256. The complete resource data and map match byte-for-byte (SHA-256 `570acaaf94dfc7ad41a6cc55432c67edbf7201259bf4767a1fe99e04a798bf14` and `7404b8d6b2e49d412520400109b6ee42814965b326c9bfe0ee256e0b19c226c1`). This does not establish independent extraction of the inner VISE payload.

Production expansion produces the exact same 3,248,871-byte KPK2 stream and all 18 records, SHA-256 `1e78c7f6fec506fbf1581c701046d6fb8a23d0691efe82d66998367cd99f6b7c`. Fresh gameplay replays of the wrapped download on the immutable candidate above pass five assertions on each CPU: 68K at 2,330 frontend / 2,930 guest ticks and PPC at 2,933 / 2,933, with zero exhausted frames. Both final screenshots are byte-identical to the first source's corresponding screenshots and were visually inspected. All three repairs #5109, #5114 and #5117 are now merged. Browser and public qualification remain pending.
